Classify each grant/VGF/milestone contribution, subsidy, availability/service payment or other public support according to contractual and
lender treatment as construction funding, operating revenue/CFADS, contingent support or another category. Prevent one support payment
from reducing construction funding need and also inflating operating CFADS/debt capacity unless expressly permitted.

### T2-S10-437 — test: ppp_security_cashflow_recovery_value
For limited-recourse debt, distinguish accounting PPE/replacement cost from enforceable lender recovery. Reconcile project cash flows,
assigned contracts/accounts, security, step-in/substitution, termination rights and asset residual value; challenge any debt-recovery
assumption that equates book asset value to recoverable collateral.

### T2-S10-438 — test: ppp_fx_convertibility_transferability
Where foreign-currency debt, hedge payments or distributions depend on local-currency project cash, separately test exchange-rate risk and
the ability to obtain and transfer/repatriate foreign currency. Reconcile guarantees/PRI/offshore reserves/convertibility facilities to caps,
triggers, process, delay, eligible payments and expiry.

### T2-S10-439 — test: userpay_rampup_liquidity_support
Where a user-pay project has a standby/ramp-up liquidity facility, model its availability period, draw conditions, permitted use, cap, fee/
interest, ranking/repayment, expiry/revolving status and downside exhaustion. Do not treat contingent support as unrestricted permanent cash
or project revenue.

### T2-S10-440 — test: userpay_concession_fee_financing
For a concession/franchise requiring upfront or recurring concession, permit or revenue-share payments to the authority, reconcile timing
and calculation to project sources/uses and operating cash flow. Identify debt/equity/tariff implications and prevent double counting with
a separate benefit-sharing mechanism where they are the same obligation.

### T2-S10-441 — test: ppp_financing_market_depth_bankability
Where financing is not yet fully committed, reconcile debt/equity funding assumptions to current project-relevant market evidence for
amount, tenor, currency, risk appetite and lender/investor capacity. Challenge a model that is ratio-bankable but depends on unavailable
market depth or assumed lender competition; replace market-sounding assumptions with binding terms as the project matures.

### T2-S10-442 — test: ppp_financial_close_affordability_recheck
For an authority model, compare preferred-bid affordability with the final financial-close payment after all procurement-permitted
reference-rate, inflation, timing, scope or financing adjustments. Confirm the adjusted payment remains within the approved funding
envelope and do not compensate bidder-retained financing components that the procurement left at risk.

### T2-S10-443 — test: ppp_authority_payment_budget_funding
For material government payment obligations, map service payments, capital contributions and expected support to the applicable long-term
budget/funding and appropriation/authorisation mechanism. Distinguish contractual obligation from annual cash appropriation and reflect any
resulting authority payment/credit risk rather than assuming decades of payment are funded by the next annual budget.

### T2-S10-444 — test: ppp_variation_pricing_lender_consent
For a material PPP variation, reproduce the contractual pricing route — such as rate card, open-book, benchmark, competitive quote or expert
process — and reconcile capex, O&M/lifecycle, financing and payment consequences. Test lender/security-agent consent and draw/covenant
impact where the finance documents make those rights relevant.

### T2-S10-445 — test: ppp_operating_invoice_to_cash_reconciliation
For a live government-payment PPP, bridge service-period entitlement through deductions/adjustments, invoice or certification, disputes,
tax, due date, cash receipt and late-payment/true-up mechanics. Reconcile operating actuals and receivables where available; do not equate
a correct payment formula with same-period cash collection.

### T2-S10-446 — test: ppp_delay_loss_liquidated_damages_recovery
For a material construction delay, quantify gross project delay loss separately from contractual D&C/EPC LD recovery. Reconcile eligible
days, rate, caps, relief/exclusions, security/credit and collection timing, then identify the residual project loss and any interim
liquidity/covenant effect. Do not assume LD entitlement perfectly offsets project delay economics.

### T2-S10-447 — test: ppp_live_risk_register_forecast_bridge
For a project in construction or operation, reconcile material current risk-register/technical/contract-management exposures to the current
forecast and downside cases, including owner, status/probability, mitigation, timing/cost/service impact and contractual relief/recovery.
Preserve the contractual base case where required and prevent double counting with contingency, claims or separate scenarios.

### T2-S10-448 — test: ppp_exit_service_continuity_transition_cost
Where the authority bears continuity responsibility at expiry or termination, reconcile interim operations, reprocurement, mobilisation,
staff/systems/data transfer, permits/spares and unrecovered remediation to the authority cash-flow case. Do not assume handback itself
provides a replacement operator and uninterrupted service for zero cost.

### T2-S10-449 — test: infrastructure_economic_vs_financial_appraisal_perimeter
Where EIRR/ENPV or social CBA is modelled, distinguish project-company financial cash flows from economic resource costs/benefits,
transfers and externalities under the governing appraisal methodology. Prevent investor NPV/IRR from being relabelled as economic return
and do not impose another institution's discount/shadow-price assumptions universally.

### T2-S10-450 — test: infrastructure_economic_without_project_counterfactual
Reconcile the economic appraisal to a credible without-project counterfactual containing the infrastructure/service activity that would
occur absent the proposal. Calculate incremental physical and economic effects versus that counterfactual rather than versus zero and
challenge benefits or avoided costs that would have arisen anyway.

### T2-S10-451 — test: infrastructure_economic_benefit_attribution_double_count
Trace each material economic benefit to a physical service change and its prescribed valuation method. Identify overlap between user
willingness-to-pay and tariff revenue, accessibility/time and land-value effects, resource savings and embedded benefit measures, or other
duplicate monetisations; resolve according to the governing CBA methodology rather than a generic exclusion rule.

### T2-S10-452 — test: infrastructure_economic_financial_sustainability_bridge
Where the decision case claims both economic viability and sustainable service delivery, bridge economic benefits to tariff/charge policy,
affordability/collection, subsidy/support, operating cash and lifecycle/renewal funding. Confirm positive economic value is not treated as
proof of financeability and financial profitability is not treated as proof of net social value.

### T2-S10-453 — test: ppp_operating_model_financial_close_equivalent_calibration
For a post-close model built in a different operating structure, reset or map the applicable financial-close inputs/opening position and
reconcile material P&L, cash flow, balance sheet, debt, lender-ratio and shareholder outputs to the agreed financial-close model before
attributing differences to actual performance. Document deliberate structural/timing changes; do not hide conversion drift in the reforecast.

### T2-S10-454 — test: ppp_operating_actual_balance_sheet_opening
For a live concession, reconcile the current forecast opening position to the latest reliable actual balance sheet and cash/debt/reserve
records. Preserve the separate contractual base case where legally required; do not use forecast financial-close cash, debt or working
capital as today's opening position merely because it is embedded in the original model.

### T2-S10-455 — test: ppp_actual_reforecast_total_contract_bridge
Bridge financial-close/base-case expectation through actual outturn to the reporting date and the current remaining reforecast, then
reconcile the resulting total-contract service payments, operating/lifecycle costs, liquidity, debt, key lender ratios and sponsor returns.
Require material differences to be attributable to actual performance, approved events, financing changes or current assumptions.

### T2-S10-456 — test: ppp_actual_cashflow_classification_lender_management
For material operating actuals, reconcile their classification across financial statements, base-case/financial-close reporting, lender CFADS
and management reporting. Confirm one transaction is not omitted, duplicated or moved to a more favourable covenant category solely
because report formats differ; the finance-document CFADS definition controls lender treatment.

### T2-S10-457 — test: ppp_opening_working_capital_cash_unwind
Trace material opening receivables, payables, accruals, prepayments and other current balances into their actual future settlement/reversal
timing after the actual-to-forecast cut-over. Challenge arbitrary first/last-period unwind, disappearance of opening obligations or double
counting between actual cash and forecast cash.

### T2-S10-458 — test: ppp_actual_shareholder_return_continuity
For a current sponsor-return or stake-value calculation, preserve dated actual equity and shareholder/subordinated-loan contributions,
repayments, interest/fees and distributions from inception through the reporting date, then append future forecast cash flows. Where
material, maintain sponsor/instrument-specific history and do not recast actual cash timing using the present reforecast.

### T2-S10-459 — test: ppp_actual_lender_ratio_history_continuity
For an operating PPP, reconcile historic CFADS, debt service, debt balances, reserve movements and covenant measurements to actual
records for completed debt-service periods, then connect the actual closing position to forward ratios. Do not overwrite historic lender
performance with a current forecast; apply the executed finance-document definitions.

### T2-S10-460 — test: ppp_staged_partial_service_payment_commencement
Where payment can start by facility, section, package or partial service before whole-project completion, reproduce the contractual
qualifying measure, acceptance date, eligible quantity/capacity, indexation and deductions for each stage. Do not use capex/spend progress
as a payment proxy unless the contract expressly does so, and do not impose all-or-nothing COD where staged payment rights exist.

### T2-S10-461 — test: ppp_periodic_lender_update_bridge
For a recurring lender operating-model submission, reconcile the current version to the prior lender-recognised/audited operating version
or agreed baseline. Explain material changes in actual financial position, assumptions, contract events, model logic, financial statements
and lender ratios so the change in project credit is traceable rather than merely a file-version difference.

### T2-S10-462 — test: ppp_management_reporting_operating_model_reconciliation
Where a separate PPP management-reporting model or layer is relied upon, reconcile material monthly actual, financial-close/base-budget
and current-reforecast P&L/cash-flow/balance-sheet and key operational outputs to the operating model and latest accounts. Allow different
presentation categories but challenge unexplained economic differences between lender, board and operating views.

### T2-S10-463 — test: ppp_distress_restructuring_stakeholder_bridge
For a distressed restructuring, bridge pre-restructuring liquidity/covenants through debt rescheduling/resculpting, sponsor support,
operating/lifecycle efficiencies and any payment/scope change to the post-restructuring lender, authority and shareholder positions. Do
not treat longer maturity or resculpting as free value and do not import historical target ratios; use the negotiated/executed cure package.

### T2-S10-464 — test: ppp_secondary_market_equity_interest_valuation
For purchase or sale of a PPP/SPV stake, compare net transaction consideration with the remaining sponsor economic interest, including
ordinary equity, shareholder/subordinated debt cash flows, fees/interest, redemption and residual obligations as applicable. Reconcile
transfer/retention restrictions, change-of-control/consents, acquisition financing, transaction costs, current operating/lifecycle/handback
risk and any refinancing/hedge effect. Use a return/discount basis consistent with the modelled cash flows; do not import the book's
illustrative return or sale premium.

### T2-S10-465 — test: ppp_contract_archetype_economic_boundary
Classify the private-participation arrangement by actual functions, asset ownership, capex/O&M responsibility, customer interface,
payment source, commercial risk and expiry/handback. Distinguish service/management contracts, lease/affermage, concession, BOT-family,
JV and hybrid structures; do not impose project-finance or concession mechanics merely from a PPP label.

### T2-S10-466 — test: ppp_lease_affermage_revenue_capex_boundary
For lease/affermage structures, reconcile customer collections, lease/affermage fee, retained operator revenue, authority-retained major
investment and operator O&M/collection risk. Test late-term maintenance/condition incentives and prevent authority capex or operator
customer-credit risk from being assigned to the wrong party.

### T2-S10-467 — test: ppp_funding_vs_financing_distinction
Reconcile the ultimate service funding source — user tariff, authority payment, subsidy, grant or regulated revenue — separately from
upfront debt/equity financing. Prevent debt/equity draws from being treated as economic project revenue or private finance from being
presented as eliminating the authority/user funding burden.

### T2-S10-468 — test: userpay_tariff_structure_reset_mechanism
Where tariff design/reset is material, reproduce customer/usage classes, cost-to-serve, elasticity, pass-through, indexation, periodic reset
and extraordinary-review rules with actual triggers, methodology, lags, caps/floors and true-ups. Do not convert a review into an automatic
return uplift or ignore a contractual reopener.

### T2-S10-469 — test: userpay_subsidy_crosssubsidy_output_based_aid
Classify general, targeted, connection, consumption, cross-subsidy, capital and output-based support. For output-based aid, reconcile
eligible customer/output, verification, certification, payment lag and continuing performance conditions; prevent subsidy from becoming
uncapped cost reimbursement or being counted before the required output is delivered.

### T2-S10-470 — test: brownfield_workforce_local_participation_obligations
For brownfield infrastructure, reconcile transferred staff, redundancy/pension/legacy employment obligations, staffing/localisation and
local ownership/content requirements to capex, opex, transition timing and eligible financing. Exclude obligations retained by government
and prevent immediate workforce optimisation where law/contract restricts it.

### T2-S10-471 — test: project_finance_structuring_vs_risk_analysis
Identify whether the model is structuring a proposed financing or testing an executed financing. In structuring mode, reconcile debt terms
to project risk and payment/bid economics; in risk-analysis mode, hold executed debt terms unless a stated restructuring scenario applies.
Do not silently resculpt debt merely to cure a failing downside.

### T2-S10-472 — test: project_finance_coverage_horizon_break_even
Interpret DSCR, LLCR and PLCR against their matching debt-service, loan-life and project-life cash-flow horizons. Where a simplified
break-even reduction is used, compare `(ratio-1)/ratio` with the full model and identify fixed-cost, tax, reserve, tariff/deduction or default
non-linearities that make the simple measure unreliable.

### T2-S10-473 — test: project_finance_multitranche_sculpting_constraints
For multiple sculpted/cash-flow-sized debt issues, reconcile tranche-specific tenor, rate/fees, capture rights, amortisation, average life,
balloons, WHT/PRI/guarantee costs, reserves and hedge requirements. Do not allocate aggregate debt service pro rata where tranche economics
differ; identify which gearing/coverage/tail/average-life/market constraint actually binds.

### T2-S10-474 — test: project_finance_resource_case_debt_sizing
Where lender debt sizing references P50/P90/P99 or other probabilistic resource/output cases, reproduce the exact lender case and CFADS
perimeter. Keep probabilistic resource cases distinct from arbitrary sensitivities and do not import a probability case into debt sizing if
the financing documents do not use it.

### T2-S10-475 — test: project_finance_deemed_equity_noncash_costs
Reconcile actual sponsor cash equity to any lender-defined equity/project-cost credit for pre-COD income, development/owner fees,
shareholder loans, capitalised financing cost, grants or other non-cash items. Prevent one amount from being counted as external project
spend, sponsor cash equity and sponsor income simultaneously.

### T2-S10-476 — test: project_finance_dsra_cash_vs_lc
Where DSRA may be cash-funded or provided by LC/reserve facility, reconcile required amount, funding/collateral, provider credit, fee,
draw, expiry, reinstatement, replenishment and release. Prevent LC support from being treated as unrestricted cash and cash DSRA from
being counted as distributable while reserved.

### T2-S10-477 — test: project_finance_mra_reserve_vs_maintenance_spend
For a major-maintenance/lifecycle reserve, separate reserve deposits/releases from the actual maintenance/renewal expenditure and its
physical timing. Reconcile CFADS/tax treatment and prevent double-counting reserve funding and lifecycle spend as two economic costs unless
the finance documents require both cash outflows.

### T2-S10-478 — test: project_finance_cash_sweep_risk_appropriateness
For cash sweeps and lock-ups, distinguish stress-period distribution traps from good-period cash capture. Test sweep benefit under the
project's actual volatility/downtrend scenarios and do not credit a sweep as automatic protection where there is little excess cash to
capture or cash flow is already stable.

### T2-S10-479 — test: project_finance_debt_service_shortfall_arrears
Where CFADS cannot meet scheduled debt service, reproduce reserve/liquidity draws, unpaid principal/interest, default interest, cure/waiver,
mandatory prepayment, acceleration or enforcement as applicable. Prevent scheduled debt service shortfalls from disappearing or later cash
from bypassing actual arrears.

### T2-S10-480 — test: project_finance_hedge_profile_average_life
Reconcile construction draws, amortisation, cash-sweep/prepayment and debt maturity to hedge notional and tenor. Use average debt life only
as a summary/approximation; test extension, contraction and over-/under-hedging plus breakage on early repayment, refinancing or termination.

### T2-S10-481 — test: project_finance_stepup_margin_refinancing_case
Where debt margins step up, retain the executed step-up in the no-refinancing case and separately model any refinancing assumption with
availability, fees, hedge breakage, reserves and PPP gain-sharing. Do not delete a contractual margin step-up merely because refinancing is
commercially desirable.

### T2-S10-482 — test: construction_actual_remaining_cost_finance
For a live construction project, reconcile actual/certified spend, WIP/accruals, current cost-to-complete, actual debt/equity/public funding,
undrawn legally available commitments and remaining support. Reprofile only the remaining construction and funding under the executed
funding rule; do not rescale historic actuals to preserve original gearing.

### T2-S10-483 — test: construction_delay_type_spend_profile
Distinguish pure completion/acceptance delay after substantial spend from delayed physical construction progress. Apply the corresponding
capex, site/escalation, IDC and revenue consequences rather than automatically shifting or freezing the whole construction S-curve for every
delay case.

### T2-S10-484 — test: infrastructure_stage_dependent_risk_exit_value
For a sale/refinancing/valuation at an intermediate project stage, identify risks actually resolved and remaining across permits, construction,
acceptance, demand/resource, O&M/lifecycle, counterparty, regulatory, merchant and handback. Use buyer/discount assumptions consistent with
the remaining risk rather than a generic mature-infrastructure return or historical transaction multiple.

### T2-S10-485 — test: infrastructure_project_vs_equity_return_diagnostic
Keep unlevered/project return distinct from sponsor/equity return and explain leverage, taxes, fees, reserves, subordinated instruments,
distribution timing and refinancing effects. Investigate counterintuitive return relationships rather than forcing a generic leverage rule.

### T2-S10-486 — test: ppp_authority_portfolio_fiscal_exposure
For a PPP programme/portfolio model, aggregate scheduled payments and expected contingent support while preserving correlated macro,
demand, termination and guarantee risks. Reconcile annual/medium-term budget/fiscal constraints and do not assess individually affordable
projects as if authority capacity were unlimited across the portfolio.

### T2-S10-487 — test: ppp_subnational_payer_credit
Where the contracting authority is municipal/subnational or a utility, reconcile payment capacity to its actual revenues, transfers,
borrowing/fiscal limits, credit enhancement and any legally enforceable higher-government support. Do not assume sovereign credit merely
because the project is public infrastructure.

### T2-S10-488 — test: infrastructure_fragile_conflict_political_risk_pathway
Where conflict/state fragility is material, trace security, access/supply-chain, payer, permit/customs, FX transfer, expropriation/change,
insurance/PRI and lender drawstop/termination pathways. Reconcile support instruments to eligible risks, caps, tenor and claim timing rather
than applying a generic country-risk haircut or blanket DFI protection.

### T2-S10-489 — test: energy_multicomponent_tariff_backtoback_guarantees
For a multi-part PPA/offtake tariff, reconcile capacity/fixed O&M/energy or fuel/heat-rate/start-up/performance components and penalties to
physical output and project cost. Compare PPA protections with EPC/O&M/fuel-supply guarantees for timing, caps, definitions, indexation and
counterparty credit; do not assume perfect pass-through.

### T2-S10-490 — test: ppp_hybrid_jv_role_conflict
For public-private joint ventures or hybrids, decompose shareholder, regulator, authority/offtaker, grantor and operator roles and the
component payment/risk allocation. Challenge tariff, dividend, subsidy or investment assumptions that rely on government acting in one role
while ignoring conflicting duties in another.

### T2-S10-491 — test: ppp_legal_regulatory_economic_enforceability
For each material modelled tariff/payment/approval/security/termination right, confirm the responsible authority/regulator and legal route
can implement it and that required permits/licences/foreign ownership/FX-transfer conditions are consistent with the model. Convert only
material unresolved institutional constraints into timing/cash-flow risk; do not apply a generic legal-risk haircut.

### T2-S10-492 — test: ppp_unsolicited_direct_negotiation_market_test
Where a PPP is unsolicited or directly negotiated, challenge any claim that proponent capex, tariff/payment, financing or return is market-
tested. Reconcile value-for-money/commercial reasonableness to the jurisdiction's permitted benchmark, independent evidence or competitive
challenge, keeping any IP/development compensation separate from core project economics.

### T2-S10-493 — test: infrastructure_equity_promote_irr_flip_waterfall
Where sponsor/investor distributions change at return hurdles, reproduce dated contribution/distribution basis, hurdle metric, cumulative
tracking, incremental allocation, catch-up/clawback and treatment of shareholder debt/refinancing/sale proceeds. Prevent constant ownership
percentages from overriding contractual flips and keep developer promote return distinct from project return.

### T2-S10-494 — test: energy_lcoe_cost_driver_benchmark
For generation projects, reconcile LCOE/levelised cost and per-unit cost benchmarks to consistent net capacity/energy, nominal/real basis,
asset life, discount basis and included capex/O&M/fuel/lifecycle/grid/carbon scope. Explain differences through project-specific technology,
resource, geography and operating drivers rather than applying generic industry averages.

### T2-S10-495 — test: userpay_connection_affordability_collection_design
Where network-service take-up is constrained by connection cost or collection design, separate connection/access charge from recurring
tariff, reflect approved subsidy/instalment/service-tier mechanisms and quantify prepaid/payment-point/billing changes where material.
Reconcile any continuing informal/small-provider service to addressable demand rather than assuming every unserved household becomes a
paying concession customer.

### T2-S10-496 — test: public_project_entity_sustainability_perimeter
For sovereign/public infrastructure, distinguish economic viability, intrinsic project financial viability and entity/service financial
sustainability. Identify the actual O&M entity and test the appropriate network/service perimeter rather than assuming project-level
viability proves sustainable service delivery.

### T2-S10-497 — test: public_network_unconstrained_om_budget_execution
For a budget-funded public operator, compare technical/normative **unconstrained** network O&M and replacement needs with budget
allocation and actual execution. Fail any conclusion based only on the small size of incremental project O&M relative to total budget
where the existing network is materially underfunded.

### T2-S10-498 — test: public_om_backlog_asset_life
Where O&M funding/execution is below required levels, trace maintenance backlog into asset condition, service, rehabilitation/renewal
need and economic life. Do not preserve full design life and service quality without an evidence-based mitigation path.

### T2-S10-499 — test: public_responsible_om_agency_funding
Identify the entity or entities responsible for post-completion O&M. Where construction responsibility and O&M responsibility differ,
assess the latter's recurrent funding/fiscal capacity and inter-agency transfer mechanism.

### T2-S10-500 — test: public_utility_network_financial_sustainability
For a corporatised/public utility, test whole-network historic and projected sustainability using relevant tariff, collection, loss/NRW,
subsidy, cross-subsidy, debt and replacement drivers. Do not infer sustainability from the new project's standalone returns.

### T2-S10-501 — test: predictable_vs_discretionary_support
For a project described as intrinsically financially viable, identify whether cost recovery depends on predictable/enforceable project
cash flows or on annual/discretionary subsidy. If discretionary support is required, test government fiscal capacity and classify the
project as support-dependent rather than self-financing.

### T2-S10-502 — test: institutional_real_project_vs_nominal_entity_basis
Where the governing development-finance methodology uses separate real project evaluation and nominal entity financial analysis,
confirm each cash-flow stream, inflation basis, tax treatment, presentation currency and discount/capital-cost measure are internally
consistent. Do not mix real FIRR cash flows with nominal capital cost.

### T2-S10-503 — test: incremental_project_financial_counterfactual
Where a project financial cost-benefit/FIRR evaluation is required, confirm expansion/replacement cash flows are incremental to a
credible without-project case. Include required O&M, replacements/periodic maintenance and closure/restoration on the prescribed
perimeter and avoid financing items the methodology expressly excludes.

### T2-S10-504 — test: public_tariff_toll_achievability
For material tariff/user-fee/toll assumptions in a public/project financial-sustainability case, test legal/regulatory authority, price-setting
objective, historic adjustment behaviour, affordability/elasticity, cost-recovery basis and cash collection. Challenge automatic model-
solving tariff escalation unsupported by the governing framework.

### T2-S10-505 — test: project_wacc_not_equity_hurdle_or_vfm
Where WACC is used for project financial evaluation, trace it to the project financing plan and relevant economic cost of each source,
including fees and opportunity cost where the methodology requires it. Keep it distinct from sponsor equity hurdle, social discount rate,
PSC/VfM discount rate and entity corporate WACC.

### T2-S10-506 — test: financial_switching_value_resilience
Where FNPV/FIRR or equivalent project-financial thresholds are used, calculate or review switching values for the most material drivers
where useful. Use the correct threshold/perimeter and do not interpret the switching value as a probability of default.

### T2-S10-507 — test: correlated_financial_risk_scenarios
Identify materially correlated financial-risk drivers and test them jointly in plausible scenarios where appropriate, such as construction
delay with cost escalation/delayed benefits or tariff increases with demand response. Avoid additive double counting and non-diagnostic
'kitchen-sink' stress cases.

### T2-S10-508 — test: public_financial_covenant_controllability
For public/development-finance covenants, confirm each material covenant is measurable, monitorable, achievable and materially within
the responsible entity's control. Do not covenant external demand, legislative appropriation or independent regulator decisions as though
they were controllable actions.

### T2-S10-509 — test: public_covenant_type_and_early_warning
Distinguish one-time conditions, continuing performance covenants and prior-consent restrictions. Where forecast non-compliance emerges,
identify the intended early-warning/mitigation path and do not use waivers or model assumptions as automatic cures.

### T2-S10-510 — test: implementation_financial_reevaluation_trigger
Where material design, scope, financing, tariff/support, O&M entity or going-concern conditions change, determine whether the governing
authority/lender methodology requires financial re-evaluation and update the viability/sustainability conclusion accordingly.

### T2-S10-511 — test: electricity_utility_support_and_loss_sustainability
For a public electricity operator, connect tariff setting/revision, renewable-support obligations, government compensation, technical and
commercial losses, metering, billing/collection and subsidy receipt to operator sustainability. Do not assume a bankable generator tariff
means the purchasing utility can fund it.

### T2-S10-512 — test: public_road_toll_retention_and_om_funding
For public/toll roads, determine whether tolls are retained by the operator or remitted to government. If retained, test toll adequacy for O&M/
capital service; if remitted, test budget allocation/execution against unconstrained road-network O&M rather than treating gross tolls as
operator cash.

### T2-S10-513 — test: social_service_recurrent_fiscal_capacity
For social/urban service infrastructure where full user-pay cost recovery is not realistic, test recurrent service/O&M funding and the relevant
government or purchasing-agency fiscal capacity. Do not treat construction financing as proof that long-run service delivery is funded.

### T2-S10-514 — test: vfm_relative_value_affordability_separation
For a PPP/public procurement comparison, confirm the alternatives meet equivalent minimum service/output requirements, distinguish
VfM from affordability, and reject 'cheapest = best' logic where service/value differs materially.

### T2-S10-515 — test: vfm_stage_purpose_and_comparator_currency
Identify whether the VfM analysis is an early screen, gateway, negotiation benchmark, bid sanity check or ex-durante/ex-post review. Confirm
the comparator is current enough for that purpose and has been updated where scope, timing, market pricing or risk allocation changed.

### T2-S10-516 — test: vfm_nonvalued_effects
Where the governing VfM methodology includes qualitative/non-valued effects, identify material performance, flexibility, competition,
deliverability or other effects not monetised and explain how they influence the decision. Do not omit them or assign arbitrary cash values
solely to force an NPV result.

### T2-S10-517 — test: vfm_risk_discount_double_count
Map each material VfM risk to the prescribed treatment — cash-flow adjustment, discount-rate adjustment or other methodology — and prevent
the same risk from being priced twice or omitted. Do not import another jurisdiction's discount-rate convention.

### T2-S10-518 — test: vfm_competition_bid_sanity
Where procurement-stage VfM relies on competition or a bid sanity check, assess the quality of market tension and compare final bids to the
appropriate updated reference/conventional option. Flag a stale or uncompetitive benchmark as weak evidence rather than proof of VfM.

### T2-S10-519 — test: ppp_operational_payment_model
For a live authority-pays PPP, reproduce current periodic payment from base payment through indexation, availability/performance adjustments,
pass-throughs/true-ups, compensation events and invoice timing. Do not administer the contract from an unadjusted bid payment.

### T2-S10-520 — test: ppp_operational_userpay_sustainability_and_review
For a live user-pay PPP with tariff resets, profit/revenue sharing or financial-equilibrium mechanisms, use actual demand/tariff history and
current forecasts to reproduce the contract's review/reset mechanics and test long-run concession sustainability.

### T2-S10-521 — test: ppp_periodic_healthcheck_savings_and_insurance
Where periodic contract review applies, test technical/financial/legal performance, market-testing/benchmarking only where contractually
appropriate, refinancing/insurance opportunities and whether proposed savings preserve maintenance, lifecycle, service quality and handback.

### T2-S10-522 — test: ppp_change_cumulative_impact_and_financing
For material PPP changes, trace the full direct and knock-on consequences, assess cumulative amendments since signature and identify the actual
source/obligation for incremental financing. Do not assume the original debt package can fund later capex.

### T2-S10-523 — test: ppp_end_contract_liability_security
For expiry/termination, test service continuity, technical/remedial plan, information/staff transfer where applicable, final settlement and the
security for late-life remedial/handback obligations. Flag residual liabilities that materially exceed remaining project cash with no reserve,
bond, escrow or equivalent support.

### T2-S10-524 — test: road_model_maturity_and_subsidy_boundary
For a road/highway PPP, confirm model depth matches the decision stage and separate investment subsidy from operating subsidy. Where currency
or intra-year timing is material, reject single-currency/annual simplifications as adequate bankable evidence without a justified bridge.

### T2-S10-525 — test: geothermal_resource_confirmation_bankability
For geothermal project finance, establish whether commercial resource confirmation/testing is sufficient for the assumed debt stage. If resource
risk remains, identify who funds/bears it and trace resource shortfall into plant size, capex, schedule, tariff and financing.

### T2-S10-526 — test: geothermal_phase_financing_and_control
Map preliminary survey, exploration, confirmation drilling, wellfield development, plant construction and operations to the party funding,
owning and controlling each phase. Preserve long-term reservoir/wellfield risk even where the public sector de-risks early exploration.

### T2-S10-527 — test: geothermal_resource_well_plant_capacity_bridge
Bridge reservoir/resource potential to production/injection well capacity, steam/fluid gathering, plant gross output, auxiliary/losses and net
metered PPA capacity/energy. Prevent thermal resource MW, nameplate MW and saleable MW from being used interchangeably.

### T2-S10-528 — test: geothermal_well_productivity_and_capex_scope
Where material, reconcile required output to well productivity, production/injection well count, drilling/gathering/interconnection scope and
future field reinvestment. Flag fixed well capex totals with no physical bridge to contracted capacity.

### T2-S10-529 — test: geothermal_resource_decline_makeup_wells
Separate reservoir/resource decline from surface-plant degradation/outage. Where decline is expected, test make-up-well triggers, cost, timing,
success/resource restoration and effect on net generation and project cash flow.

### T2-S10-530 — test: geothermal_capacity_energy_tariff
Where the PPA separates capacity and energy charges, link capacity payment to contractually available/accepted capacity and energy payment to
net dispatched/delivered energy and actual variable components. Apply weighting, indexation, availability and penalty mechanics and avoid a
generic thermal-fuel pass-through where inapplicable.

### T2-S10-531 — test: geothermal_offtaker_credit_concentration
For a geothermal/IPP project with one material PPA payer, distinguish demand protection from counterparty payment risk. Reconcile PPA exposure
to utility credit enhancement, sovereign support, LC/liquidity support and their expiry/call mechanics.

### T2-S10-532 — test: technology_project_maturity_stage
For a project that depends on unproven or early-commercial technology, identify the actual engineering/technology maturity at the model date and reconcile the precision of capex, schedule, performance and financing assumptions to that maturity. Flag lender-grade certainty supported only by conceptual or early pilot evidence.

### T2-S10-533 — test: technology_physical_kpi_evidence_bridge
Trace each material technology cash-flow driver — throughput, yield, quality, uptime, efficiency, degradation or analogous KPI — to representative test, pilot, demonstration, guarantee or operating evidence. Distinguish measured performance from extrapolated commercial assumptions.

### T2-S10-534 — test: foak_scaleup_and_integration_gap
For FOAK or early-NOAK assets, compare the scale and integration of demonstrated operation with the proposed commercial plant. Identify unsupported scale-up steps, untested unit-operation interfaces and assumptions that treat component success as full-system performance.

### T2-S10-535 — test: technology_cost_estimate_maturity
Reconcile the current capex and contingency to the actual engineering definition and supplier/EPC evidence. A late-stage financing case should not retain a precise early estimate when FEED, site integration or major equipment pricing remains unresolved.

### T2-S10-536 — test: technical_commercial_finance_maturity_convergence
Assess whether engineering, site/permits, supply/offtake contracts, O&M, insurance and financing have matured consistently. Flag a project that is technically advanced but commercially unsupported, or commercially contracted before the technical design can credibly meet the contract.

### T2-S10-537 — test: technology_contract_maturity_matrix
Classify each material contract as discussion, LOI/MOU, term sheet, binding offer or executed agreement and test whether the model applies rights that exist only in later-form documents. Pay particular attention to termination, price, liability, assignment and credit-support terms.

### T2-S10-538 — test: project_entity_separateness_and_parent_linkage
For a stand-alone project-finance case, identify material parent/SPV linkages including cross-default, affiliate contracts, cash pooling, shared liabilities, IP/O&M dependence and reorganisation rights. Do not infer bankruptcy remoteness from the SPV label alone.

### T2-S10-539 — test: technology_epc_actual_risk_allocation
For the construction package, map design, price, schedule, technology, integration and performance risk to the executed/expected EPC terms. Ignore the EPC/LSTK/GMP/EPCM label where exclusions, change rights, liability caps or weak security materially alter the supposed wrap.

### T2-S10-540 — test: feedstock_supply_specification_and_process_fit
Where feedstock/resource is material, reconcile contracted quantity, quality/specification, price, logistics, substitution and supplier credit to the process assumptions. Test the cash effect of off-spec, unavailable or replacement feedstock rather than stressing price alone.

### T2-S10-541 — test: equipment_supplier_epc_interface
For specialist equipment outside the core EPC, identify responsibility for design integration, delivery, commissioning, warranty and performance. Flag any gap where supplier and EPC warranties are individually valid but no party is responsible for integrated output.

### T2-S10-542 — test: technology_insurance_and_recovery_perimeter
For technology-performance insurance or guarantees, trace trigger, deductible, exclusions, cap, policy term, claim timing and use of proceeds. Prevent full double recovery from EPC LDs, warranty, insurance and other credit support unless the contracts explicitly permit it.

### T2-S10-543 — test: technology_development_budget_and_funding
Before financial close, map remaining FEED, pilot, site, permitting, adviser, diligence and other development costs to committed development capital. Do not use permanent project debt before it is legally available or assume every sunk sponsor cost is reimbursable.

### T2-S10-544 — test: financing_source_project_fit
Where debt remains uncommitted, compare required quantum, tenor, currency and risk acceptance with current project-specific lender/market evidence. Historical market ranges or the theoretical existence of a financing product are not proof of executable capital.

### T2-S10-545 — test: technology_equity_rights_and_strategic_economics
Where multiple equity classes or strategic investors exist, reconcile capital, preferred rights, control, dilution, distribution priority and affiliate commercial benefits. A strategic investor's total economics may include EPC, equipment, feedstock, offtake, O&M or tax/incentive value beyond stated equity return.

### T2-S10-546 — test: pfi_deduction_passdown_liability_cap
For an operating PFI/availability PPP, compare authority deductions with the responsible subcontractor's liability and liability cap. Quantify the portion that remains at SPV level after caps, exclusions and recovery timing.

### T2-S10-547 — test: latent_construction_defect_recovery_tail
For material operating defects, verify that D&C warranty/limitation rights, bond/guarantee and contractor solvency still support the assumed recovery. Flag recovery assumed after the relevant contractual or security period has expired.

### T2-S10-548 — test: disputed_deduction_attribution_and_liquidity
Where deductions/remediation are disputed among service, lifecycle and construction parties, separate current cash outflow from ultimate legal/economic allocation. Test whether the SPV can remain liquid while responsibility is unresolved.

### T2-S10-549 — test: lifecycle_handback_distress_capacity
For a project nearing expiry, compare credible remaining lifecycle/handback/remediation cost with restricted lifecycle funding, remaining project cash and legally available sponsor/support value. Flag a late-life funding gap even if historic investor IRR is strong.

### T2-S10-550 — test: service_provider_replacement_distress_cost
If a service provider may fail or be terminated, model replacement procurement, mobilisation, price premium, temporary deductions, transition cost and recovery/security. Do not replace the provider instantaneously at the original contract price.

### T2-S10-551 — test: limited_recourse_no_automatic_rescue_equity
In a distress case, identify committed versus merely possible sponsor funding. Do not inject unlimited equity to avoid insolvency unless that support is contractually committed or is an explicitly labelled rescue scenario.

### T2-S10-552 — test: contract_model_vs_lender_operating_model
For a live PFI/PPP, distinguish the contractual financial-close/base-case model from the current lender/operational model and any shareholder return model. Use the current operating model for solvency/covenant assessment while preserving the contract model for calculations that require it.

### T2-S10-553 — test: remaining_shareholder_value_as_shock_absorber
Calculate or inspect remaining future shareholder cash flows and committed sponsor support rather than relying only on lifetime IRR. Low remaining distributions can reduce rescue incentives and available economic headroom.

### T2-S10-554 — test: historic_irr_not_forward_distress_capacity
Challenge a project presented as financially healthy because investor IRR remains high after earlier dividends/refinancing. Test current liquidity, remaining shareholder value, reserves, lifecycle and debt headroom independently.

### T2-S10-555 — test: reserve_underfunding_distress_signal
Compare actual DSRA, lifecycle/MRA and other reserve balances with finance-document targets and replenishment rules. Treat a reserve shortfall as a potential stress signal and do not count unfunded reserve cash as unrestricted strength.

### T2-S10-556 — test: lender_waiver_default_and_reporting_signals
Where lender reports are available, identify waiver requests, default notices, covenant certificates, material-adverse-effect notices and restructuring activity. A waiver changes the immediate remedy, not the underlying economics, unless its terms genuinely cure the issue.

### T2-S10-557 — test: statutory_accounts_distress_reconciliation
For an operating project in material stress, reconcile going-concern disclosures, provisions, overdue liabilities, auditor comments and reporting delays to the current model. Investigate material uncertainty in accounts that is absent from the lender/operating forecast.

### T2-S10-558 — test: authority_deduction_distress_strategy
For serious PFI distress, quantify the service-continuity and financial effect of full contractual deductions versus any proposed temporary suspension/settlement/cure strategy. Preserve contractual rights and do not assume maximum immediate deductions necessarily maximise authority value.

### T2-S10-559 — test: road_automatic_subsidy_is_not_commitment
Where a road model solves operating subsidy to a target ADSCR/DSCR, classify the output as a funding-gap diagnostic unless government support is legally and fiscally committed. Quantify the support requirement and replace the plug with the actual support mechanism for a bankability case.

### T2-S10-560 — test: road_vehicle_class_toll_and_cost_bridge
Reconcile vehicle-class traffic, class tolls, discounts/exemptions and collection to revenue, and connect traffic to material variable operating cost. Do not use one blended traffic × tariff calculation where class economics are material.

### T2-S10-561 — test: road_periodic_heavy_maintenance_cash_timing
Distinguish annual accounting expense/accrual from actual periodic resurfacing or major-maintenance cash. Confirm the financing/reserve can meet the cash requirement in the intervention year.

### T2-S10-562 — test: road_public_authority_cashflow_perimeter
For an authority road case, separately show investment/operating subsidies, toll-related taxes/fees and retained public costs/support. Do not infer public value from tax receipts while omitting contingent guarantees or service obligations.

### T2-S10-563 — test: road_distress_threshold_jurisdiction_specific
Reject legacy teaching-model insolvency tests or fixed IRR/coverage hurdles as universal rules. Apply the actual concession, financing and local legal/financial-distress framework.

### T2-S10-564 — test: port_existing_new_terminal_phasing
For a concession combining an existing terminal with new-build or rehabilitation, separate operating revenue, construction/rehab outages, migration and new-terminal COD. Test cannibalisation and common-cost/capacity allocation.

### T2-S10-565 — test: port_container_mix_tariff_discount
Where material, segment gateway/transshipment and full/empty container flows and apply the correct handling/storage tariff, index and customer discount. Prevent a transshipment discount from reducing unrelated traffic.

### T2-S10-566 — test: port_fixed_variable_labour_and_currency
Reconcile core staffing and traffic-linked variable labour to throughput and operating windows, preserving relevant local/foreign currency exposures. Throughput growth with flat labour cost requires evidenced productivity/spare capacity.

### T2-S10-567 — test: port_concession_fee_components
Decompose port authority payments into fixed rent, indexed components, throughput fee, revenue royalty, minimum guarantee or other actual bases. Stress fixed versus variable components correctly in a throughput downside.

### T2-S10-568 — test: port_eca_trustee_and_revolver_separation
Where export-credit/ECA debt, a trustee/DSRA and revolving liquidity coexist, test each instrument's distinct eligibility, restriction and repayment mechanics. Do not count restricted debt-service cash as working capital or an ECA facility as general-purpose funding.

### T2-S10-569 — test: port_related_party_contractor_sponsor_return
If a sponsor is also EPC contractor, equipment supplier or operator, separate SPV project economics from sponsor-group EPC/O&M/equipment margin and equity/shareholder-loan return. Test related-party pricing and avoid double-counting affiliate value.

### T2-S10-570 — test: technology_ip_stepin_and_operating_continuity
For proprietary technology, verify that project/lender rights to the IP, documentation and technical support survive the relevant sponsor default/step-in scenario for the needed period. Physical ownership of equipment is not sufficient if operation legally or practically depends on terminated IP rights.

### T2-S10-571 — test: technology_bankability_not_project_irr_only
A high project or equity IRR does not prove technology-project financeability. Reconcile return to demonstrated performance, contract maturity, counterparty credit, construction wrap, liquidity and debt-market fit.

### T2-S10-572 — test: offtake_term_vs_debt_and_replacement
Compare firm offtake term, termination/cure, renewal notice and replacement lead time with debt maturity/average life. Flag debt reliance on an uncommitted contract tail or optimistic renewal.

### T2-S10-573 — test: term_sheet_to_financial_close_assumption_convergence
At financial close or FID, confirm LOI/MOU/term-sheet assumptions have been replaced by executed contract and financing terms where available. Quantify any value or coverage change caused by final liability, price, schedule, hedge or reserve terms.

### T2-S10-574 — test: technology_downside_causal_combination
Run at least one coherent technology-project downside linking physical underperformance to commercial and financing consequences, including applicable recoveries and caps. Avoid isolated sensitivities that never test the interaction between yield/availability, contracts, liquidity and debt.

### T2-S10-575 — test: pfi_financial_stress_early_warning_composite
For a distressed operating PPP, combine forward coverage, reserve funding, remaining shareholder value, lifecycle/handback exposure, material disputes, subcontractor credit and lender/going-concern evidence into an integrated assessment. Do not declare the project healthy or insolvent from one ratio or one accounting metric.

### T2-S10-576 — test: ppp_reverse_revenue_requirement_scoping
For an early-stage PPP scoping model, bridge required service/output and rough whole-life/project-finance cost to the approximate revenue requirement, then test feasible user charges, public payments and support. Treat the exercise as a gap diagnostic, not a revenue plug to an investor return, and replace illustrative ratios/returns as project evidence matures.

### T2-S10-577 — test: ppp_do_minimum_and_technical_alternatives
Where the model supports project selection rather than only an executed contract, reconcile the preferred PPP scope against the applicable do-minimum/defer/rehabilitate and materially different technical/site alternatives. Confirm the financial case does not assume the selected solution is necessary before service-equivalent options are screened.

### T2-S10-578 — test: ppp_enabling_infrastructure_funding_boundary
Identify material retained-authority or third-party enabling works such as access roads, utilities, grid/network reinforcement, land or intermodal connections. Reconcile owner, funding, completion date and contractual delay consequence to project COD/demand; do not show the SPV as fully deliverable if an essential external work has no credible funded path.

### T2-S10-579 — test: ppp_environmental_social_cost_schedule_bridge
For material legal, lender or contractual environmental/social obligations, trace assessment/mitigation/compensation/monitoring requirements into project capex, opex, land, programme and financing conditions. Do not import another jurisdiction's thresholds; use the actual applicable requirement.

### T2-S10-580 — test: ppp_consent_permit_financing_dependency
Where a permit, community/land consent or safeguard approval gates site access, debt draw, construction or operation, confirm the model timing and downside reflect the unresolved status and cure path rather than assuming unconditional approval on the base date.

### T2-S10-581 — test: ppp_related_party_operator_contractor_economics
Where sponsor affiliates provide EPC, O&M, management, supply or other project services, separate the SPV payment/cost from affiliate margin and sponsor investment return. Reconcile related-party pricing/approval requirements and correlated sponsor-counterparty risk; prevent the same affiliate economics being counted as both project saving and extra return.

### T2-S10-582 — test: project_finance_constrained_gearing_selection
Where a model claims maximum or optimal leverage, identify the binding DSCR/LLCR, maturity/tail, minimum-equity, completion-support, rating, market-capacity, reserve or other financing constraint. Confirm the chosen structure lies in a feasible financing set rather than maximising debt percentage mechanically.

### T2-S10-583 — test: project_finance_bank_vs_bond_cash_timing
For a genuine bank-versus-bond financing comparison, reconcile construction draw/prefunding, negative carry, coupon/IDC, amortisation flexibility, reserves/covenants, maturity, rating/trustee costs, prepayment/breakage and refinancing/termination effects. Compare all-in cash economics, not headline coupon alone.

### T2-S10-584 — test: project_finance_index_linked_debt_basis_and_accretion
Where debt is inflation/index linked, reconcile its index, base/reset lag, coupon and principal accretion to the revenue index/reset, O&M/lifecycle inflation and amortisation. Under material inflation cases confirm debt fully repays and distributions do not leak cash needed for accreted principal.

### T2-S10-585 — test: project_finance_credit_wrap_value_and_scope
Where a guarantee, wrap or bond insurance is used, identify covered events/amounts/periods, guarantor credit/call conditions, fees and related obligations, then reconcile the actual improvement in pricing, tenor, debt capacity or market access. Do not treat partial-risk/political-risk cover as an all-risk guarantee.

### T2-S10-586 — test: project_finance_sharia_asset_based_cashflow_perimeter
Where financing is Sharia-compliant or asset/profit based, model the executed ownership, purchase/resale, lease/rental, profit-share or redemption cash flows and their ranking/security rather than converting them into a generic interest line. Reconcile construction, tax/transfer and early-termination consequences to the actual documents.

### T2-S10-587 — test: project_finance_project_lease_residual_handback
For a project lease/asset-finance structure, identify legal owner, lessee rights, rent, purchase option/residual value and end-of-term transfer. Confirm terminal sale/purchase proceeds do not conflict with project-agreement handback and that insurance/maintenance/termination cash follows the party bearing the risk.

### T2-S10-588 — test: project_finance_blended_sponsor_capital_return
Where sponsor capital includes ordinary equity, shareholder/subordinated debt or preferred instruments, preserve instrument-specific advances, service/redemption and ranking in sponsor returns. Explain any headline equity-IRR uplift through the full sponsor cash-flow bridge rather than treating it as project-level value creation.

### T2-S10-589 — test: ppp_refinancing_current_distribution_npv_bridge
Where the contract calculates refinancing gain from projected sponsor distributions with and without the refinancing, use the same current pre-refinancing operating assumptions in both cases and the contract's valuation/discount basis. Include only permitted costs/effects and prevent double counting of equity, shareholder debt and transaction costs.

### T2-S10-590 — test: ppp_refinancing_equity_extraction_and_audit_rights
Where refinancing reduces sponsor equity exposure or produces immediate cash extraction, apply any special contractual sharing/consent rule and reproduce material authority information/model-audit rights. Do not use an ordinary gain-share formula if the contract treats early degearing/extraction differently.

### T2-S10-591 — test: hospital_ppp_scope_and_responsibility_boundary
For a hospital/health PPP, classify building/lifecycle, equipment, clinical staff, administration, ancillary services, IT, procurement and billing responsibilities by party. Confirm the financial model charges and revenues follow that scope and does not assume every hospital PPP is project-financed or privately operated end-to-end.

### T2-S10-592 — test: hospital_demand_case_mix_and_capacity
For a demand-exposed hospital model, bridge catchment/demographics/disease/referrals/competition to inpatient, outpatient, diagnostics/procedures, length of stay, capacity and payer/case mix. Do not use one bed-occupancy assumption as proof of all activity or revenue.

### T2-S10-593 — test: hospital_payer_reimbursement_working_capital
Separate self-pay, insurer, public-payer, grant/service-payment and uncompensated-care cash. For material claim payers, model eligibility, submission/denial/return and cash lag into receivables and liquidity; do not equate recognised reimbursement revenue with immediate cash.

### T2-S10-594 — test: hospital_payment_compensation_mechanism
Identify whether the hospital private party earns management fee, cost reimbursement, case/activity payment, mark-up, revenue/profit share, availability payment or a hybrid. Reproduce eligible revenue/cost and true-up rules and prevent mutually exclusive bid options or overlapping fees/shares from being paid together.

### T2-S10-595 — test: hospital_clinical_administrative_equipment_interface
Where the private scope includes hospital management, reconcile administration, staffing, drug/supply inventory and medical-equipment availability to clinical service capacity and quality. Challenge cost savings that leave service, accreditation and revenue unchanged despite reduced critical inputs.

### T2-S10-596 — test: hospital_licensing_accreditation_revenue_eligibility
Map material operating licences, specialty/professional and payer/insurer accreditations to renewal dates, responsible party and the services/reimbursements they enable. Confirm a lapse has the correct service, cost and revenue consequence rather than full operations continuing automatically.

### T2-S10-597 — test: hospital_staff_transition_and_double_count
Where a PPP changes the employment model, bridge existing staff into retained, transferred, redeployed, retired or rehired categories, including transition/severance/recruitment/training cost where applicable. Prevent legacy public payroll and full private replacement payroll from both remaining unless both are genuinely incurred.

### T2-S10-598 — test: hospital_contract_performance_monitoring_financial_link
For hospital KPIs used in the PPP contract, map evidence/measurement to threshold, cure/remediation and payment/default/accreditation effect. Do not invent penalties for clinical metrics outside the contract, but do not leave contract-linked quality failures as financially inert dashboard items.

### T2-S10-599 — test: hospital_system_internal_flow_and_scale
Where the PPP covers multiple hospitals, reconcile shared procurement/overhead, central services, referrals and capacity transfers across facilities. Prevent internal transfers from being counted as new system demand and confirm receiving-site capacity/cost responds to service reallocation.

### T2-S10-600 — test: hospital_public_health_need_vs_private_revenue
For a health PPP justified by service/access outcomes, keep public-health need separate from billable private demand. Where tariffs are unaffordable or reimbursement is insufficient, expose the actual subsidy/service-payment/funding requirement rather than plugging patient charges to meet investor return.

### T2-S10-601 — test: social_housing_revenue_structure_and_unit_perimeter
For social/affordable/student housing treated as infrastructure, classify development-sale, rental, availability-supported or mixed tenure. Trace units/beds through availability, occupancy, rent/fees, concessions/arrears and collection; prevent sold units from continuing to earn rent absent a genuine leaseback/management right.

### T2-S10-602 — test: project_vs_holdco_debt_structural_subordination
Where project and holdco debt coexist, reconcile project covenant/reserve/distribution capacity to cash actually available upstream for holdco service. Do not use holdco debt to improve SPV bankability, and model the holdco downside when project distributions are locked or delayed.

### T2-S10-603 — test: tollroad_forecast_maturity_stage
Identify the traffic forecast's purpose, date and maturity: pre-feasibility, authority procurement case, bidder case, lender/investment-grade case or operating update. Confirm the financial model does not rely on an early screening forecast as if it had transaction-grade survey, model-validation and due-diligence support.

### T2-S10-604 — test: tollroad_forecast_building_block_bridge
Decompose material traffic into existing, reassigned, diverted/capture, underlying growth, development, induced and other network/policy effects where supported by the traffic study. Check that future traffic components are not double counted and that the financial model preserves the traffic study's economic perimeter.

### T2-S10-605 — test: tollroad_opening_day_bankable_traffic
For a greenfield/materially changed road, isolate opening-day/in-scope demand from long-term speculative growth. Test whether early debt service depends on currently observable/capturable traffic or on later development/induced demand that cannot reasonably support the first debt-service periods.

### T2-S10-606 — test: tollroad_survey_program_annualisation
Review the traffic study's classified counts, O-D, travel-time and network surveys, survey timing/duration and annualisation. Challenge known holiday, event, tourism-season, construction or abnormal-weather observations being extrapolated to a normal year without a credible adjustment or longer-duration benchmark.

### T2-S10-607 — test: tollroad_trip_matrix_vehicle_time_segments
Where a travel-demand model is material, reconcile trip matrices and network demand by relevant vehicle/user class and time slice. Confirm the forecast does not collapse materially different peak/off-peak, car/heavy-vehicle or origin-destination behaviour into one aggregate that changes toll capture or revenue.

### T2-S10-608 — test: tollroad_network_model_validation
Inspect evidence that the travel-demand model represents material competing routes, speeds, capacities, junctions and travel times and has been calibrated/validated appropriately. Flag a model described as validated only against the same observations used to force calibration or with material residual mismatch left unexplained.

### T2-S10-609 — test: tollroad_generalized_cost_capture
Where traffic capture is based on route choice, reconcile travel time, value of time, vehicle operating cost, toll and any supported reliability/safety/convenience effect to the project's alternatives. Confirm the resulting capture/diversion logic is economically consistent with the toll-road benefit offered to each material user group.

### T2-S10-610 — test: tollroad_wtp_rp_sp_evidence
For material willingness-to-pay assumptions, identify whether evidence is revealed preference, stated preference or another method; reconcile sample and segmentation to actual users; and test how the result enters toll-diversion/capture. Do not accept one unsupported average WTP as proof of demand across heterogeneous traffic classes.

### T2-S10-611 — test: tollroad_toll_strategy_capture_consistency
Reconcile toll points, distance basis, class tariffs, discounts/exemptions, tax, payment method, escalation and timing between the traffic study and financial model. If the finance model changes the toll strategy materially, require a corresponding traffic response or an explicit documented assumption explaining why capture is unchanged.

### T2-S10-612 — test: tollroad_rampup_debt_overlap
Reconcile ramp-up depth and duration to greenfield/brownfield status, user awareness, opening readiness and comparable-project evidence. Stress the periods where ramp-up overlaps peak debt balances and test reserve, liquidity, support, covenant and default consequences if ramp-up is slower than forecast.

### T2-S10-613 — test: tollroad_socioeconomic_driver_independence
For long-run traffic growth, identify material GDP/income, employment, population, car-ownership, fuel-price, mobility and land-use drivers. Challenge double counting among correlated drivers, use of aspirational macro targets as base assumptions and transfer of short historical relationships unchanged across a multi-decade concession.

### T2-S10-614 — test: tollroad_development_induced_separation
Where development or induced traffic is material, trace development status/timing and trip-generation assumptions separately from ordinary socio-economic growth and reassignment. Prevent the same trips being counted as development, induced and underlying growth or being assumed before the relevant development/access exists.

### T2-S10-615 — test: tollroad_competing_complementary_timing
Reconcile the assumed opening, widening, deterioration, tolling or traffic-management of material competing and complementary infrastructure to the traffic forecast and contract. Test correlated downside where a feeder/complementary project is late or a competing route improves earlier than expected.

### T2-S10-616 — test: tollroad_toll_policy_escalation_risk
For regulated/authority-approved toll increases, distinguish contractual formula from actual approval/political implementation risk. Test delay, cap or non-implementation where credible rather than assuming every permitted toll escalation is automatically collected on the formula date.

### T2-S10-617 — test: tollroad_fx_toll_currency_boundary
Where tolls are local-currency but debt/cost or toll escalation has FX exposure, reconcile which party bears currency, convertibility and tariff-reset risk. Do not label traffic risk transferred if the private party is actually protected from traffic but exposed to an unmodelled FX/toll basis, or vice versa.

### T2-S10-618 — test: tollroad_weather_climate_traffic_uncertainty
Where seasonal weather or climate hazards materially affect traffic, annualisation, road closure or competing-route availability, reflect the supported traffic and cash consequences separately from physical repair/insurance effects. Challenge a climate-exposed road with weather-sensitive demand but no traffic-seasonality or disruption case.

### T2-S10-619 — test: tollroad_unforeseen_revenue_shock
Where credit resilience is material, run or review a discrete traffic/revenue shock for unforeseen events separately from the ordinary low case, so its effect can be identified. Reconcile any force-majeure or supervening-event relief to the actual contract rather than assuming every demand shock is compensated.

### T2-S10-620 — test: tollroad_optimism_outside_view
For a material demand-risk forecast, compare key assumptions such as opening capture, ramp-up, growth and WTP with relevant external/outturn evidence. Treat a strong inside-view forecast without comparable-project challenge as a risk indicator, not as an automatic error or a reason to impose a generic reference-class haircut.

### T2-S10-621 — test: tollroad_strategic_misrepresentation_incentive
Assess whether authority, bidder or sponsor incentives reward higher traffic forecasts through approval, concession fee, lower subsidy, winning bid or valuation. Where the incentive is material, require independent due diligence and test whether financial viability survives a less aggressive traffic case.

### T2-S10-622 — test: tollroad_winners_survivors_curse
For a procurement with high traffic uncertainty, assess whether the selection process could favour the most optimistic bidder and whether only projects with positively skewed forecasts survive screening. Test whether realism criteria, comparable evidence and financing commitment reduce these selection biases.

### T2-S10-623 — test: tollroad_public_forecast_benchmark
Where government is allocating material traffic risk, confirm it has a credible independent traffic benchmark or equivalent basis to evaluate bidder forecasts and public liabilities. Reconcile any third-party/PPP-unit/public-financier review of the forecast to the assumptions used in the authority financial case.

### T2-S10-624 — test: tollroad_shared_baseyear_bidder_model
Where a validated base-year travel-demand model is supplied to bidders, distinguish common observed/network inputs from bidder-owned future forecasts and assumptions. Do not treat use of the government base model as a guarantee of bidder future traffic, nor allow undisclosed post-bid model changes to escape comparison.

### T2-S10-625 — test: tollroad_lender_traffic_adviser_case
Where project debt relies on traffic, identify independent lender traffic-adviser work and the traffic case actually approved for credit. Reconcile debt sizing/covenants to the lender/debt case rather than automatically using the sponsor base case, and explain material traffic-case changes between bid and financial close.

### T2-S10-626 — test: tollroad_bid_forecast_realism
Where procurement evaluates traffic-risk bids, test whether unusually high traffic forecasts are subject to realism checks/penalties or equivalent safeguards and whether multiple floating bid parameters obscure economic comparison. Do not assume the highest concession fee or lowest subsidy is best if it is driven by unsupported demand.

### T2-S10-627 — test: tollroad_traffic_risk_index_screen
Use asset type, trip mix, project need, user benefit, tolling history, connectivity, macro environment, development/network dependency, FX/toll stability, preparation quality, financier due diligence, legal enforceability and bidder asymmetry as a qualitative traffic-risk screen. Do not convert illustrative scores or cut-offs into a probability of default or universal rating.

### T2-S10-628 — test: tollroad_shadow_bid_financial_model
For an authority shadow-bid model, bridge authority traffic cases to toll/payment revenue, operating/lifecycle cost, CFADS, financing capacity, debt-service headroom, sponsor return and required toll/support/term. Confirm the model is used to understand bankability/affordability rather than presented as a prediction of the winning bid.

### T2-S10-629 — test: tollroad_scenario_credit_bridge
For each material traffic low/base/high case, trace vehicle-class traffic through toll/payment revenue and CFADS into the actual DSCR/LLCR or lender metrics, reserve use, lock-up and default/cure path. Use transaction thresholds, not illustrative ratios from sector guidance.

### T2-S10-630 — test: tollroad_structuring_nexus
Assess the selected road structure against risk transfer, bankability and user/government affordability simultaneously. If the structure works only by transferring unmanageable traffic risk, imposing unaffordable toll/support or retaining excessive public exposure, test alternative scope/phasing/payment/risk-allocation options rather than a plug.

### T2-S10-631 — test: tollroad_availability_retained_traffic_risk
For an availability road, confirm private revenue is performance/availability based while any retained public toll/demand exposure remains on the authority side. Test authority payment affordability and payment credit; do not claim traffic risk has disappeared merely because it is outside SPV revenue.

### T2-S10-632 — test: tollroad_blended_availability_structure
Where the private party earns both availability payment and toll revenue, reconcile each cash stream, offset/true-up, traffic exposure, government payment risk, toll-setting rights and upside sharing. Classify the structure as hybrid rather than pure availability or full user-pay.

### T2-S10-633 — test: tollroad_shadow_toll_traffic_revenue_split
For shadow tolls, separate measured traffic risk borne by the private party from user-tariff, evasion, collection and currency risks retained by the authority. Where real tolls are also collected, keep authority user receipts distinct from contractual shadow-toll payments to the SPV.

### T2-S10-634 — test: tollroad_shadow_toll_banding
Where shadow tolls use traffic bands, calculate marginal payment by band and test low/base/high traffic against both SPV debt/equity cash and authority affordability. Do not replace a descending or nil upper-band structure with one average per-vehicle tariff.

### T2-S10-635 — test: tollroad_mrg_early_debt_protection
Where an MRG is used, identify whether its purpose is to protect debt during opening/ramp-up or provide broader revenue support. Reconcile floor, period/expiry and debt tenor so the model does not preserve full equity upside where the contract only provides a limited lender-oriented floor.

### T2-S10-636 — test: tollroad_mrg_contingent_liability
Stress the government's payment under the MRG using coherent low-traffic cases, aggregate caps and actual payment timing/credit. Report the support as a contingent fiscal exposure rather than free project revenue, and test payment risk if the authority/sovereign is financially constrained.

### T2-S10-637 — test: tollroad_cap_collar_revenue_sharing
Where a revenue floor is paired with an upside share/cap, model both limbs, their common revenue definition, reference forecast, timing and true-up together. Prevent downside support being included while the authority's contractual upside recovery is omitted.

### T2-S10-638 — test: tollroad_public_subordinated_support
For public/mezzanine or subordinated traffic-risk support, reconcile draw, ranking, deferral, repayment, pricing, intercreditor/security rights and downside loss. Test whether it crowds in senior/private capital or merely substitutes public funding and whether grantor/investor role conflicts are managed.

### T2-S10-639 — test: tollroad_full_userpay_risk_transfer
For a full user-pay concession/lease, verify strong in-scope demand, WTP/user benefit, policy stability and downside resilience support genuine traffic-risk transfer. For brownfield monetisation, compare the up-front concession value with the foregone public toll stream and challenge valuations dependent on aggressive traffic assumptions or expected renegotiation.

### T2-S10-640 — test: tollroad_flexible_term_liquidity
For a flexible-term/revenue-target road concession, reconcile cumulative-revenue target mechanics to the moving expiry date, senior debt maturity/tail, ramp-up liquidity, added-years lifecycle and handback. Confirm term extension does not falsely cure an early debt-service shortfall.

### T2-S10-641 — test: tollroad_operating_outturn_reforecast
For an operating toll road, bridge actual-versus-forecast traffic/revenue into ramp-up, capture/toll, growth, development/induced, network, collection/policy and one-off variances where material. Use the outturn to recalibrate future forecasts without rewriting the original historical forecast.

### T2-S10-642 — test: tollroad_traffic_study_financial_model_trace
Trace the approved traffic study scenario/version through vehicle-class periodic traffic, ramp-up/growth, tolls/discounts/exemptions, chargeable transactions, cash collections, traffic-driven O&M/lifecycle/capacity and financing outputs. Identify any manual financial-model traffic overrides or post-study uplifts as separate assumptions requiring evidence and downside testing.

### T2-S10-643 — test: tollroad_toll_elasticity_revenue_curve
Where the traffic case uses toll elasticity, test both traffic and revenue response across the relevant toll range by material user class. Challenge revenue optimisation that assumes one constant elasticity outside the evidence range or raises toll above the point where the traffic response makes revenue fall without showing that effect.

### T2-S10-644 — test: tollroad_traffic_adviser_evidence_package
For a transaction-grade demand-risk road, confirm the traffic evidence package is proportionate to risk and includes the relevant survey/data report, base-year model, capture/diversion methodology, development/validation evidence, forecast assumptions, annualisation/ramp-up, low/base/high cases, sensitivity ranking and due-diligence support. Do not impose illustrative survey durations or sample percentages from sector guidance as universal thresholds.

### T2-S10-645 — test: tollroad_matrix_estimation_bias
Where material O-D movements are synthetically filled through matrix estimation, compare prior and final matrices, trip lengths/ends and validation results. Test whether synthetic trips or subsequent growth materially inflate project capture and treat heavy unobserved matrix filling as opening-day traffic risk rather than observed demand.

### T2-S10-646 — test: energy_efficiency_economic_bargain
Reconstruct baseline/counterfactual utility or operating cost through ECM performance, verified savings, financing/ESCO payment and retained host benefit. Confirm savings are not a free-standing revenue plug.

### T2-S10-647 — test: energy_efficiency_baseline_counterfactual
Test whether the pre-project baseline and reporting-period counterfactual reflect material weather, occupancy, production, operating-hours and facility changes using the agreed adjustment method.

### T2-S10-648 — test: energy_efficiency_audit_maturity
Classify the technical evidence from screening/benchmark through detailed or investment-grade audit and commissioning. Confirm the evidence maturity is sufficient for the capital and financing decision being made.

### T2-S10-649 — test: energy_efficiency_evidence_package
Trace utility/meter data, equipment condition, O&M, operating schedules, site measurements, ECM costs and interactions into the model assumptions. Identify unsupported savings assumptions or excluded measures.
